# Code map: default.xbe

An index of the default.xbe functions and addresses the project relies on: what each one does,
how sure we are, and where it's used or written up. Look here before tracing a function; it may
be done already. Addresses are virtual addresses in the US `default.xbe` (base 0x10000; `.text`
0x12000-0x3118b0, `.rdata` from 0x386340, `.data` from 0x3b5b00).

* `FUN_0022c4d0` is an address where Ghidra made a function: its C is in
  `decompiled/xbe/ghidra/c/` (`python tools/fn.py 22c4d0` prints it). `0x22d0b0` is code Ghidra
  didn't split out: read it in `decompiled/xbe/default.text.asm`. Data (tables, vtables,
  globals) is written `0x3bd9d4`; `tools/xbe_peek.py f|i|x ADDR` reads values. Search the page
  for an address's hex digits without `0x` or leading zeros (`22c4d0`, `7c720`) to find it in
  either form.
* `bit N` is a bit index (bit 0 is mask 1, bit 3 is mask 8); `flag 0x40` is a mask.
* A row covers a function; the places inside it that a page cites, and the constants it reads,
  are in that row. The constants also have their own table at the end.
* Name tables are `{length, name, value}` records. Their address here is where the game's text
  parser starts reading: the first name pointer, 4 bytes past the first record.
* Offsets: `char+0x490` is a character object's field, `type+0x220` its character type's
  (`[char+0xc]`), `skill+0x1c` its skill object's (`[char+0x5a4]`), `weapon+0x218` a weapon
  object's, `data+0xd0` the weapon data a weapon points at from +0x228.

**How sure** (the third column):
* **code**: read from the disassembly or Ghidra's C (static, not run).
* **live**: also checked in the running game (xemu's gdb stub, or a take that matches it).
* **data**: read from the game's data files (`decompiled/xml/`), with the code that uses it.
* **inferred**: deduced from its use, the data or the footage, not read line by line.
* **guess**: a reading nobody has checked.

Brackets narrow a word: the part that is only inferred, or the source's own rating (medium,
low).

**Where** (the fourth column): a docs page and its section, a source file under `viewer/src/`
(`play_shots.rs` for `src/bin/play_shots.rs`), a ticket. `todo/35` leads, spec or notes are the
research in the git-ignored `todo/35-*/` folder; `todo/damage-factor` is the lead's code
question in `todo/damage-factor/answer.md`. "This page" means it was read for this index.

**Adding a row.** Put it in its area's table, at its address. One line: what it does, with the
game's own names (enum names, strings, attribute names); how sure; where it's used. When a row
is wrong, correct it and say so in your report. When two places disagree, read the code, record
what it does in the row and say which place was wrong.

STANDARD -4, HARD 0, BRUTAL 4. The default game flags hold it at -4: `common/default-gameflags.xml` `<campaign>` lists it, the decoder naming its hash h_1a98de0a `shadoon-look` (a collision with `difficulty-setting`), so a session built from them reads STANDARD. The demo: STANDARD on the missions and the test map, 0 on the multiplayer maps. | code (n = -4 in the campaign: read live, #129; 0 in a split-screen match: read live, mechanism untraced) | todo/damage-factor; play.md § Damage; `play_damage.rs` (`Difficulty::for_session`) |

The sweep counter is byte +3 (3 from FUN_00225000's reset), counted down each tick; at 0 the ray (FUN_000ddd10) runs from the last checked point (+0x70) over the way moved plus this tick's step, then +3 = 3: ticks 3, 6, 9 ... from the launch, 0.1 s apart. No hit: +0x70 = the position. A hit: the bolt set 0.1 % short of it, the body gathered, the bolt ended (flag 4) unless it passes or ricochets; FUN_00224770 deals it in the same tick (damage, hole, effect, sound) and the next tick releases the bolt. Between sweeps nothing is tested, so a bolt flies on into a wall for up to two ticks. The pool ticks after the objects' update in FUN_000d9a20 (0xd9ae2, then 0xd9b07), so the launch frame would be tick 1; the takes count from the frame after (answers-batch1.md §1). This state (+0x68, +0x6c, +0xa1, +0xa0 bit 7) is the controller's, the player's: a hand-over (FUN_000c8fa0) leaves it, so a saved pair comes back on the new member. **LT** (VB 7, 0x13460e-0x134a30): nothing selected (0x134612, before the lock's test): "Inventory is empty" (h_1393ebea) each frame LT is held while +0x68 is 0 or -1 (0x1349c3: so 3 s from the release), +0x68 = -1 held or not (0x13451b, 0x1349df); LT reading 0 clears +0xa0 bit 7 (0x13467e); +0x68 another stack (or -1): the charge zeroed, +0x68 = 0 once LT is up (0x1346a7), nothing used; FUN_000abbb0 refusing: its message for 3 s at the press (unless bit 7), +0x68 = -1 (even with LT up, 0x13497f: so the next frame clears it again, and a press landing on such a frame shows nothing; inferred as the cause of #196's two silent presses at full health, take13 / take15); bit 7 set (a use made with LT still down): +0x68 = -1 (0x1346f6, 0x134957); else LT down: +0x68 = the stack (0x134751), a non-grenade used at once (charge 0) and bit 7 set (0x1348af), a grenade charged at 1.6 a second (0x3a5088) to FUN_000abf30's cap, the meter; LT up with a charge: thrown at clamp((charge - 0.3) x 1 / 0.7, 0.01, 1) (0x3a50b0, 0x3a54c8). ## Effects / ALE

Materials and shaders are here too.

| `FUN_0001d0c0` | Builds a frame from a direction (y up for a level one): the explosion turned along a missile's way (FUN_0022cbe0), a decal's frame (FUN_0022ca20). A hit effect's alignment 2 (FUN_0021a850) builds it from the stored surface normal; FUN_0022cbe0 from the hit's velocity. | code | play.md § Missiles, § Bolts and their hits; todo/73 answer-round4 §Q2; todo/weapons-plan/answers-batch1.md §2; `play_missile.rs` (`split`); `play.rs` (`hit_frame`: Bevy's `looking_to`) |

| `FUN_00065800` | ALE emitter update. Count = trunc(elapsed x Emitter_Frequency), + InitialParticles on the first update, + the EmitCount keys in [t_last, t], then trunc(Emitter_LODCurve(LOD +0x8c) x count) (0x659ed-0x65a12). t_last is held while the count is 0 and the frequency isn't; on emitting, t_last = t and the fraction is lost. Runs once a game frame: cut each update, its count depends on the step. Node fields: +0x30 Frequency, +0x34 InitLifeSpan, +0x38 Pressure, +0x3c EmitCount, +0x40 VelocityApproach, +0x44 InitialParticles, +0x48 LODCurve. | code | todo/73 answer-round3 §3.2; play.md § Missiles; `ale_fx.rs` (`game_count`, `EMIT_LOD`, `GAME_FRAME`) |

## AI

| Address | What it does | How sure | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FUN_000124f0` | Sets a brain timer (vtable 0x3a4024, whose tick 0x124a0 counts it down by the AI dt; the brain's timer list at brain+0xb0, vtable 0x3a47b0, is ticked by 0x12470 at the top of each AI update, 0x1b0d7): it overwrites what was left. The reactions' cooldowns are such timers: brain+0xd4 KNOCKDOWN, +0xe4 STUN, +0xf4 DIVE, +0x104 DODGE. | code | todo/129 answers Q1; `play_enemies.rs` (`KNOCKDOWN_COOLDOWNS`, `STUN_COOLDOWNS`) |
| `FUN_00012650` | A route's first index: 0 when its entries (+4..+8, 0x18 bytes each: a position and a point) aren't empty, else -1. FUN_00026940 goes on from it at the end of a chain whose last point has both links (a loop). | code | brain.md § Patrol; `play_brain.rs` (`patrol`) |
| `FUN_00012b40` | Fills the brain's goal table: a 0x40-byte record for each of the 0x87 goal ids, +0 its start, +0x10 its end, +0x20 its update, +0x30 a fourth (0x271040 for most), each a thunk (`mov eax, [ecx]; jmp [eax + slot]`) through the goal holder's vtable 0x3a47b8. | code | brain.md; `play_brain.rs` |
| `FUN_000163f0` | The AI brain's constructor. At 0x16719 it writes the damage record's window, brain+0x238 = 2.7 s (0x402ccccd); nothing else writes it. | code | todo/129 answers Q1; `play_enemies.rs` (`DAMAGE_WINDOW`) |

| `0x694a0` / `0x69490` | The app's pause and unpause (its vtable 0x3a2a18 +0x10 / +0xc; no Ghidra functions): each forwards to the game session's (app+8) vtable +0x40 / +0x44, 0x7a320 / 0x7a340. The item list's open and close call them (FUN_000ac080, FUN_000ac160), as the item widget's update does when a second player comes (0xabcfb). | code | `play.rs` (`PAUSED`, `set_paused`); `play_itembox.rs` (`open_list`, `close_list`) |

| `FUN_0007a230` | Takes the session's queued pause requests (+0x6d8: its blocks +0x6dc, their count +0x6e0, the head +0x6e4, the size +0x6e8): a pause at depth 0 (with [0x46995c]+0x1c clear) sets +0x5bc bit 1 (paused) and pauses the sound groups (FUN_0007a360); depth +1.
